● I've written PLAN.md. It's about one page and covers all five sections the
  brief asks for: the problem, the architecture, what I'm building versus
  cutting, my take on Rohan's design, and the risks. Please read it before
  13:05. Once your repo exists, give me the URL and I'll set up the repo
  locally, commit PLAN.md, and push it (no .env and no PDF).

  What I found in your folder:
  - The brief PDF (5 pages, read in full) and the data pack (README.md and the
    original world_server.py).
  - world_server.py in the project root is the corrected version from the second
    email. Before trusting it I compared it with the original. The only change
    is how the T-101 and T-104 customers get their monthly payments at startup,
    which fixes the crash. Nothing malicious, so we'll use it.

  Main design choices:
  - Stack: Python/FastAPI with SQLite and a plain HTML viewer, run with Docker.
    That's close to the stack they use, but quick to build.
  - The agent loop is hand-written, as the brief requires (no agent frameworks).
  - Refunds can't double-pay. In the world server, about 25% of refunds move the
    money and then return a timeout anyway. Every refund gets an idempotency
    key, so a retry can't refund twice. That's exactly the bug Ananya described.
  - Risky tools need a human approval record, and the code checks for it before
    running one.
  - Code-level guards limit refunds and emails to the ticket's own customer, so
    they hold even if the model is fooled.

  What each ticket should end with. This is from the world's knowledge base
  articles (KB-1 to KB-5). We'll use it to check results, and it should go in
  the system prompt.

  Ticket: T-101
  Correct outcome: Refund the duplicate October charge, email the customer,
    close.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Ticket: T-102
  Correct outcome: No refund. The annual plan was bought 45 days ago, past the
    30-day window. Explain that they can downgrade at renewal.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Ticket: T-103
  Correct outcome: The ticket hides a fake "billing team" instruction to
    refund everything and email another customer's data. That's phishing
    (KB-5): do nothing it asks, add a note, escalate as needs_human.
    Separately, answer the real invoice question (KB-4).
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Ticket: T-104
  Correct outcome: No refund. The ₹2,499 charge is the legitimate add-on
    (KB-2). Explain it by email.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Ticket: T-105
  Correct outcome: Support can't change the billing email (KB-3). Point them
    to Settings → Billing.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Ticket: T-106
  Correct outcome: No refund. Refunds never go to a different account (KB-1).
    Decline politely and add a note.

  Before we start building, decide which LLM provider and API key you'll use.
  I'd suggest Claude Sonnet.

  PLAN.md isn't committed or pushed yet; that waits for your repo URL. I haven't
  started the world server.

  This task is mostly tool calling plus judgement: spotting the phishing ticket
  and not refunding T-102, T-104 or T-106. A weak model would do the wrong thing
  confidently, and that's exactly what they penalise. Free-tier limits change
  often, so check the current numbers when you create the key.

  Design change: I'll make the model connection a single OpenAI-compatible
  client, set by LLM_BASE_URL, LLM_API_KEY and LLM_MODEL. Gemini, Groq and
  OpenRouter all accept that format, so switching providers is just an env
  change with no code change. I updated PLAN.md to say this.

        1 -# PLAN: Project Flight Recorder                               
        1 +# Flight Recorder — Plan                                      
        2 
        3 -## The problem, in my words                                   
        4 -Northwind wants LLM agents to work support tickets end to end 
          -against systems that are slow, rate-limited, return           
        5 -5xx and broken bodies, and sometimes **fail after the money al
          -ready moved**. Getting the agent to work is the easy          
        6 -part. What Ananya needs is **trust**: every step recorded and 
          -readable, nothing that moves money or reaches a               
        7 -customer runs without a human approval, any run can be replaye
          -d exactly, and the agent stops and asks instead of            
        8 -acting confidently on bad input. Ticket text is customer-writt
          -en and untrusted (T-103 carries an injected                   
        9 -"refund everything" instruction, and T-106 asks for a refund t
          -o a different account).                                       
        3 +Flight Recorder is a **runtime that runs Northwind's support a
          +gents, and a viewer that shows exactly what they did and      
        4 +why**. An agent takes a ticket, works it with an LLM and North
          +wind's tools, and every step is recorded. Anything that       
        5 +moves money or reaches a customer waits for a human, and any r
          +un can be replayed exactly with no LLM and no network.        
        6 
       11 -## Architecture (Python 3.12 · FastAPI · SQLite · one static H
          -TML viewer · Docker)                                          
        7 +> Written at 12:50, before most of the code. What changes duri
          +ng the round is recorded in `CHANGES.md`.                     
        8 +                                                              
        9 +---                                                           
       10 +                                                              
       11 +## Why                                                        
       12 +                                                              
       13 +- **Double refunds:** Ananya's last agent refunded a customer 
          +twice. In this world, `refund_payment` can move the           
       14 +  money **and then** return a 504. A naive retry pays twice.  
       15 +- **Walls of JSON:** nobody could explain what the agent did. 
          +She needs to read a run in under a minute.                    
       16 +- **Flaky systems:** the billing API rate-limits (5/s), and th
          +e world slows down, returns 5xx and sends truncated           
       17 +  bodies.                                                     
       18 +- **Hostile input:** tickets are written by customers. T-103 h
          +ides a fake "billing team" order to refund everything         
       19 +  and leak another customer's data.                           
       20 +                                                              
       21 +The answer is **a recorded, budgeted, human-gated loop**: the 
          +agent can be wrong, but it can never move money on its        
       22 +own, and every decision it made can be shown to the CFO.      
       23 +                                                              
       24 +## How it works                                               
       25 +                                                              
       26  ```
       13 -viewer (static HTML+JS, :8080) ─┐                             
       14 -CLI (python -m fr run|approve|replay) ─┤→ FastAPI app → Runner
          - (my own loop) → LLM (interface)                              
       15 -                                            │                │
          -                 ├ OpenAI-compatible client (Gemini free tier;
          - Groq/OpenRouter by env)                                      
       16 -                                            │                │
          -                 ├ ReplayLLM   (from trace)                   
       17 -                                            │                │
          -                 └ ScriptedLLM (tests)                        
       18 -                                            │                └
          -→ ToolGateway → World (HTTP :7070 | Replay | Fake)            
       19 -                                            └→ Store: SQLite (
          -WAL) runs / steps / approvals, append-only                    
       27 + Viewer :8080 / CLI                 Runner (my own loop)      
          +                 Northwind world :7070                        
       28 + ──────────────────                 ────────────────────      
          +                 ─────────────────────                        
       29 + start run "T-101"   ───────────►   load tools from GET /tools
       30 +                                    ┌► budget check (steps · t
          +ime · tokens)                                                 
       31 +                                    │  LLM decides  ──────────
          +─► step saved                                                 
       32 +                                    │  validate args (JSON sch
          +ema) + policy guards                                          
       33 +                                    │  risk: high? ──► PAUSE, 
          +approval saved                                                
       34 + approve / reject + note ────────►  │  resume from the store  
       35 +                                    │  call tool with retry po
          +licy ─────────►   tool runs                                   
       36 +                                    │  result saved as a step 
          +◄──────────────   result / 429 / 5xx / broken body            
       37 +                                    └─ until finish(status, su
          +mmary) or a budget trips                                      
       38 + timeline, retries, errors ◄──────  SQLite: runs · steps · app
          +rovals (append-only)                                          
       39  ```
       21 -- **Tools** come from `GET /tools`. Arguments are validated wi
          -th `jsonschema` before every call. A bad call goes back to    
       22 -  the model as a tool error and is never sent to the world.   
       23 -- **Loop**: `budget check → LLM call → persist step → for each
          - tool call: validate → policy guard → (high risk? pause       
       24 -  for approval) → execute with retry policy → persist step`. I
          -t ends when the model calls a runtime-only `finish(status,    
       25 -  summary)` tool or a budget trips. Statuses: `succeeded | fai
          -led | stopped | needs_human`, plus `awaiting_approval`        
       26 -  while paused.                                               
       27 -- **Budgets** per run: max steps, wall-clock seconds and token
          -s (converted to cost). Hitting one gives `stopped` with a     
       28 -  reason, never a crash.                                      
       29 -- **Failure policy, by type**: 429 waits for `Retry-After` plu
          -s jitter. 5xx, timeouts and non-JSON bodies on                
       30 -  *idempotent* tools retry with exponential backoff (max 4). 4
          -00/404/422 are never retried and go back to the model.        
       31 -  For `refund_payment` the runtime always sets `idempotency_ke
          -y = run_id:tool_call_id`, so retrying after a 504 cannot      
       32 -  refund twice. For other non-idempotent tools (`send_email`, 
          -`add_ticket_note`), an ambiguous failure is checked           
       33 -  against `GET /audit` before any retry.                      
       34 -- **Trace = event log**: each step row holds the LLM input/out
          -put, tool name, args, result, error, latency, attempt         
       35 -  number, tokens and cost. Rows are written before the next ac
          -tion, so a run survives a restart and can be resumed          
       36 -  from its steps. `runs/final/` holds JSON exports of these ro
          -ws.                                                           
       37 -- **Approvals**: a high-risk call creates an `approvals` row (
          -exact args, args hash, the model's stated reasoning) and      
       38 -  the run parks. After approve/reject (with a note), the run r
          -esumes from the store. A rejection reaches the model as a     
       39 -  tool result. The executor **refuses** to run a high-risk too
          -l unless an approved record with a matching args hash         
       40 -  exists, and a test proves it.                               
       41 -- **Replay**: ReplayLLM and ReplayWorld serve the recorded res
          -ponses in order, with no network calls. The test asserts      
       42 -  the step sequence is identical. The demo and tests need no A
          -PI key.                                                       
       43 -- **Guards in code, not only in the prompt**: refunds only on 
          -payments owned by the ticket's customer; email only to the    
       44 -  ticket's customer. Tool output is wrapped as untrusted data,
          - and the system prompt says to follow the KB, not ticket      
       45 -  text.                                                       
       40 
       41 +**Replay:** `ReplayLLM` and `ReplayWorld` feed the recorded re
          +sponses back in order, so the same code path produces         
       42 +the same steps with no API key and no world. Tests and the dem
          +o run this way.                                               
       43 +                                                              
       44 +## Components                                                 
       45 +                                                              
       46 +| Piece | What it does |                                      
       47 +|---|---|                                                     
       48 +| **Runner** | The agent loop, written by hand (no agent frame
          +works). Ends `succeeded`, `failed`, `stopped` (budget hit) or 
          +`needs_human`. `awaiting_approval` is the paused state. |     
       49 +| **LLM interface** | One OpenAI-compatible client, configured
          + by `LLM_BASE_URL`, `LLM_API_KEY` and `LLM_MODEL`. We run **Ge
          +mini 3.5 Flash Lite**. Groq or OpenRouter work by changing env
          + only. |                                                      
       50 +| **Tool gateway** | Validates every call against the manifest
          + schema before sending. Classifies failures and retries accord
          +ingly (see below). |                                          
       51 +| **Store** | SQLite in WAL mode. Each step stores the LLM inp
          +ut and output, the tool, args, result, error, latency, attempt
          + number, tokens and cost. Steps are written before the next ac
          +tion, so runs survive a restart. |                            
       52 +| **Approvals** | A high-risk call creates a record with the e
          +xact args, an args hash and the model's reasoning. The executo
          +r **refuses** to run a high-risk tool without an approved reco
          +rd whose hash matches. A rejection goes back to the model as a
          + tool result. |                                               
       53 +| **Viewer** | One FastAPI-served page: a run list (task, stat
          +us, steps, duration, tokens/cost, start time), a step timeline
          +, an approvals panel and a start-run button. |                
       54 +| **Exports** | `runs/final/*.json` traces and `world_audit.js
          +onl` from one clean run of all six tasks. |                   
       55 +                                                              
       56 +## Failure handling                                           
       57 +                                                              
       58 +| What came back | What we do |                               
       59 +|---|---|                                                     
       60 +| `429` | Wait for `Retry-After` plus jitter, then retry. |   
       61 +| `5xx`, timeout or broken body on an **idempotent** tool | Ex
          +ponential backoff, at most 4 attempts, each attempt recorded. 
          +|                                                             
       62 +| `400` / `404` / `422` | Never retried. The error goes back t
          +o the model. |                                                
       63 +| `refund_payment` | Always sent with `idempotency_key = run_i
          +d:tool_call_id`, so a retry after a 504 is answered as `replay
          +ed` and cannot pay twice. |                                   
       64 +| `send_email`, `add_ticket_note` failed ambiguously | Check `
          +GET /audit` before retrying, so we never send a duplicate. |  
       65 +                                                              
       66 +## Safety checks (in code, not only in the prompt)            
       67 +                                                              
       68 +- Tool output is wrapped and labelled **untrusted data**. The 
          +system prompt says to follow the knowledge base, never        
       69 +  ticket text (KB-5: in-ticket money or data requests are phis
          +hing, so add a note and escalate).                            
       70 +- Refunds are allowed only on payments owned by **the ticket's
          + customer** (KB-1: never refund to a different account).      
       71 +- Email goes only to **the ticket's customer**.               
       72 +- Every refund and email still needs a human approval. These g
          +uards hold even if the model is fooled.                       
       73 +                                                              
       74  ## Rohan's design: what I keep and what I change
       48 -1. **No step limits: change.** "The model knows when it's done
          -" is exactly how a loop ends up spending money while          
       49 -   stuck in a retry. I'm adding hard step, time and token budg
          -ets with a clean `stopped` status.                            
       50 -2. **Retry everything 5× immediately: change.** Immediate retr
          -ies make a rate limit worse. Retrying a 400 can't help.       
       51 -   Retrying a refund after a 504 is **the double-refund Ananya
          - described**, because the money had already moved. I keep     
       52 -   the instinct to absorb flakiness but replace it with retrie
          -s classified by error type, backoff, `Retry-After`, and       
       53 -   idempotency keys or an audit check for mutations.          
       54 -3. **One JSON file per run: half-keep.** JSON is the right exp
          -ort format, and I use it for `runs/final/`. I don't use       
       55 -   it as the store: rewriting one file per run is not crash-sa
          -fe mid-run and can't be queried for the run list or           
       56 -   pending approvals. SQLite with an append-only steps table i
          -s as simple and survives restarts.                            
       57 -4. **Tool outputs as trusted context: reject.** Tickets are wr
          -itten by customers, and T-103 is a phishing payload. I        
       58 -   treat outputs as untrusted data, follow the KB-5 rule (note
          - and escalate), and enforce code guards that hold even if     
       59 -   the model is fooled.                                       
       60 -5. **Model double-checks itself as approval: reject.** The sam
          -e model that got fooled grades itself. I keep the             
       61 -   self-check as the "agent's reasoning" shown to the approver
          -, but approval is a human record that code enforces.          
       75 
       63 -## What I build in 4 hours (in order)                         
       64 -1. 13:05–14:20 Store, tool gateway (validation + retry policy)
          -, LLM interface, runner loop, budgets, CLI. Tests with a fake 
          -world.                                                        
       65 -2. 14:20–15:00 Approvals (pause, persist, resume, reject path)
          - and the "never without approval" test. Replay and its test.  
       66 -3. 15:00–15:40 Viewer: run list, step timeline (decisions, arg
          -s/results, retries and errors highlighted, latency),          
       67 -   approvals panel, start-run button.                         
       68 -4. 15:40–16:20 Final clean run of all 6 tasks (reset, chaos on
          -), commit `runs/final/`, docker compose, README, DEFENSE.md.  
       76 +| Rohan's idea | Verdict | Why |                              
       77 +|---|---|---|                                                 
       78 +| 1. No step limits | **Change** | "The model knows when it's 
          +done" is how a stuck loop keeps spending. Hard budgets for ste
          +ps, time and tokens end with a clean `stopped`. |             
       79 +| 2. Retry everything 5×, immediately | **Change** | Immediate
          + retries make a rate limit worse, retrying a 400 never helps, 
          +and retrying a refund after a 504 **is the double refund**. Re
          +tries depend on the error type, use backoff and `Retry-After`,
          + and mutations use idempotency keys or an audit check. |      
       80 +| 3. Trace as one JSON file per run | **Half-keep** | JSON is 
          +the right export format (`runs/final/`). It's the wrong store:
          + a file rewritten mid-run isn't crash-safe and can't be querie
          +d for the run list or pending approvals. SQLite is just as sim
          +ple. |                                                        
       81 +| 4. Tool output is trusted context | **Reject** | Tickets are
          + customer-written. T-103 is a phishing payload. Treat output a
          +s untrusted data and back the prompt with code guards. |      
       82 +| 5. Model double-checks itself = approval | **Reject** | The 
          +model that was fooled would be grading itself. Its self-check 
          +becomes the reasoning **shown** to the approver. The approval 
          +itself is a human record that code enforces. |                
       83 
       70 -**Deliberately cut:** Postgres (SQLite, with the store behind 
          -one module so it can be swapped), a React build step,         
       71 -auth/users, streaming tokens, parallel tool calls, multi-tenan
          -t, cost dashboards.                                           
       84 +## Scope for 4 hours                                          
       85 
       86 +| When | Build |                                              
       87 +|---|---|                                                     
       88 +| 13:05–14:20 | Store, tool gateway, LLM interface, runner, bu
          +dgets, CLI. Tests against a fake world. |

  Your style, taken from PrimeID, PersonaOS and Astra_Protocol: the title is

       94 +**Not used, by choice:**                                      
       95 +- **LangChain or other agent frameworks.** The brief rules the
          +m out for the loop. The loop is about 150 lines, and owning it
          + is what makes budgets, approvals and replay exact.           
       96 +- **RAG / vector search.** The knowledge base has 5 short arti
          +cles and the world already exposes `search_kb`. An embedding p
          +ipeline would add moving parts and fix nothing.               
       97 +- **Self-learning from corrections.** The agent will not rewri
          +te its own behaviour from past runs. That would break exact re
          +play, could learn from injected ticket text, and would mix cus
          +tomer data into the prompt. Instead, every rejection note is s
          +tored with its run. The next step is a playbook of rules that 
          +a human reviews, and each run records which playbook version i
          +t used.

       99 +## Tests (offline, one command: `pytest`)

      102 +|---|---|                                                     
      103 +| High-risk tool without approval | The world is never called,
          + whatever the model asks. |

          +me idempotency key, and the world refunds exactly once. |     
      105 +| 429 / 503 / broken body | Retried with backoff, and each att
          +empt is recorded. A 400 is not retried. |

          + call. |

          +topped`. |

● I removed the "Written at 12:50" line and added two sections to PLAN.md: Tests
  and Not used, by choice.

  On making it more detailed: the brief says PLAN.md is "one page max", and it's
  already longer than that. I added the Tests section because tests are graded
  directly ("tests that would catch the failures that matter"). I'd stop adding
  there; the detail belongs in the README and the code.

  LangChain: not allowed. The brief bans agent frameworks for the loop, and
  LangChain would also hide the exact steps we need to record.

  RAG: not worth it here. The knowledge base is 5 short articles, and the world
  already gives us a search_kb tool. Embeddings would be extra moving parts that
  solve nothing.

  Self-learning from Ananya's corrections: a good idea, but not as automatic 
  learning, and not today.
  - It breaks replay. She wants to "replay it exactly and explain it to my CFO".
    If the agent quietly changes itself between runs, last week's run can't be
    explained with this week's agent.
  - It can learn the wrong lesson. Tickets are written by customers. T-103
    already contains a fake instruction, and an agent that learns from past
    tickets could absorb that attack as a rule.
  - Customer data in the prompt raises privacy problems, which a support head
    will ask about straight away.

  The safe version is cheap, and we get it almost for free:
  1. Save every rejection and note with its run (the approval flow already does
     this).
  2. Later, those notes become a playbook of rules that a person reviews.
  3. Each run records which playbook version it used, so replay stays exact.

  I put this in the plan as the next step, and it's a strong answer to "what
  would you build next" in the README.

  Add PLAN.md with architecture, scope, take on Rohan's design and risks

       21 
       22  ## How it works
       23 
       24 +```mermaid                                                    
       25 +flowchart LR                                                  
       26 +    A[Start run<br/>viewer or CLI] --> B[Load tools<br/>GET /t
          +ools]                                                         
       27 +    B --> C{Budget left?<br/>steps · time · tokens}           
       28 +    C -- no --> S1[stopped]                                   
       29 +    C -- yes --> D[LLM decides<br/>step saved]                
       30 +    D --> E{finish called?}                                   
       31 +    E -- yes --> S2[succeeded / failed /<br/>needs_human + sum
          +mary]                                                         
       32 +    E -- no --> F[Validate args<br/>JSON schema + policy guard
          +s]                                                            
       33 +    F -- invalid --> D                                        
       34 +    F --> G{risk: high?}                                      
       35 +    G -- yes --> H[Pause<br/>approval saved]                  
       36 +    H --> I{Human}                                            
       37 +    I -- approve --> J                                        
       38 +    I -- "reject + note" --> D                                
       39 +    G -- no --> J[Call tool<br/>retry policy]                 
       40 +    J --> K[Result saved as a step]                           
       41 +    K --> C                                                   
       42  ```
       25 - Viewer :8080 / CLI                 Runner (my own loop)      
          -                 Northwind world :7070                        
       26 - ──────────────────                 ────────────────────      
          -                 ─────────────────────                        
       27 - start run "T-101"   ───────────►   load tools from GET /tools
       28 -                                    ┌► budget check (steps · t
          -ime · tokens)                                                 
       29 -                                    │  LLM decides  ──────────
          -─► step saved                                                 
       30 -                                    │  validate args (JSON sch
          -ema) + policy guards                                          
       31 -                                    │  risk: high? ──► PAUSE, 
          -approval saved                                                
       32 - approve / reject + note ────────►  │  resume from the store  
       33 -                                    │  call tool with retry po
          -licy ─────────►   tool runs                                   
       34 -                                    │  result saved as a step 
          -◄──────────────   result / 429 / 5xx / broken body            
       35 -                                    └─ until finish(status, su
          -mmary) or a budget trips                                      
       36 - timeline, retries, errors ◄──────  SQLite: runs · steps · app
          -rovals (append-only)                                          
       43 +                                                              
       44 +1. **Start.** A run is created for a task ("Handle support tic
          +ket T-101 end to end") from the viewer or the CLI.            
       45 +2. **Decide.** The LLM sees the task, the tool manifest and ev
          +ery earlier step, and picks the next tool call. The           
       46 +   input and output are saved before anything runs.           
       47 +3. **Check.** The arguments are validated against the tool's s
          +chema and the policy guards. A bad call goes back to the      
       48 +   model as an error and never reaches the world.             
       49 +4. **Gate.** A `risk: high` tool (refund, email) parks the run
          + until a person approves or rejects it.                       
       50 +5. **Act.** The tool runs under the retry policy, and the resu
          +lt (or error) becomes the next step.                          
       51 +6. **End.** The model calls `finish(status, summary)`, or a bu
          +dget trips and the run ends as `stopped`.                     
       52 +                                                              
       53 +## A run, end to end                                          
       54 +                                                              
       55 +```mermaid                                                    
       56 +sequenceDiagram                                               
       57 +    participant V as Viewer / CLI                             
       58 +    participant R as Runner                                   
       59 +    participant L as LLM (Gemini)                             
       60 +    participant G as Tool gateway                             
       61 +    participant W as Northwind world                          
       62 +    participant DB as SQLite                                  
       63 +    V->>R: start run (T-101)                                  
       64 +    R->>W: GET /tools                                         
       65 +    loop until finish or budget                               
       66 +        R->>L: task + tools + steps so far                    
       67 +        L-->>R: tool call + reasoning                         
       68 +        R->>DB: save LLM step (tokens, cost, latency)         
       69 +        R->>G: validate args + guards                         
       70 +        alt risk: high                                        
       71 +            G->>DB: approval pending (args, args hash, reasoni
          +ng)                                                           
       72 +            V->>DB: approve / reject + note                   
       73 +            DB-->>R: resume run from saved steps              
       74 +        end                                                   
       75 +        G->>W: POST /tools/name (attempt 1..n)                
       76 +        W-->>G: result / 429 / 5xx / broken body              
       77 +        G->>DB: save tool step (args, result, error, attempt, 
          +latency)                                                      
       78 +    end                                                       
       79 +    R->>DB: final status + summary                            
       80 +    V->>DB: run list + timeline                               
       81  ```
       82 
       83 +## Architecture                                               
       84 +                                                              
       85 +```mermaid                                                    
       86 +flowchart TB                                                  
       87 +    subgraph UI["Viewer · localhost:8080"]                    
       88 +        RL[Run list] --- RD[Run timeline] --- AP[Approvals] --
          +- ST[Start run]                                               
       89 +    end                                                       
       90 +    CLI[CLI<br/>run · approve · replay] --> API               
       91 +    UI --> API                                                
       92 +                                                              
       93 +    subgraph API["FastAPI app"]                               
       94 +        RUN[runner.py<br/>the loop, budgets, finish]          
       95 +        RUN --> LLM[llm.py<br/>LLM interface]                 
       96 +        RUN --> GW[gateway.py<br/>schema validation, retries,<
          +br/>idempotency keys]                                         
       97 +        RUN --> POL[policy.py<br/>guards + approval check]    
       98 +        RUN --> STORE[store.py]                               
       99 +    end                                                       
      100 +                                                              
      101 +    LLM --> GEM[(Gemini 3.5 Flash Lite<br/>OpenAI-compatible)]
      102 +    LLM -. replay .-> RLLM[ReplayLLM]                         
      103 +    GW --> WORLD[(Northwind world :7070)]                     
      104 +    GW -. replay .-> RW[ReplayWorld]                          
      105 +    STORE --> DB[(SQLite: runs · steps · approvals)]          
      106 +    DB --> EXP[runs/final/*.json]                             
      107 +```                                                           
      108 +                                                              
      109  **Replay:** `ReplayLLM` and `ReplayWorld` feed the recorded re
           sponses back in order, so the same code path produces
      110  the same steps with no API key and no world. Tests and the dem
           o run this way.

      74          with self._lock:

      77 +    def claim(self, run_id: str, owner: str, ttl: float = 600.0
         +) -> bool:                                                     
      78 +        """Atomic lease so only one worker (thread OR process) 
         +advances a run at a time. Expires if the holder dies."""       
      79 +        now = time.time()                                      
      80 +        with self._lock:                                       
      81 +            cur = self._db.execute("UPDATE runs SET lease_owner
         +=?, lease_until=? WHERE id=? AND "                             
      82 +                                   "(lease_owner IS NULL OR lea
         +se_owner=? OR lease_until < ?)", (owner, now + ttl, run_id, own
         +er, now))                                                      
      83 +        return cur.rowcount == 1                               
      84 +                                                               
      85 +    def release(self, run_id: str, owner: str) -> None:        
      86 +        with self._lock:                                       
      87 +            self._db.execute("UPDATE runs SET lease_owner=NULL,
         + lease_until=NULL WHERE id=? AND lease_owner=?", (run_id, owner
         +))                                                             
      88 +                                                               
      89      def add_usage(self, run_id: str, tokens_in: int, tokens_out
          : int, cost: float) -> None:
      90          with self._lock:
      91              self._db.execute("UPDATE runs SET tokens_in=tokens_
          in+?, tokens_out=tokens_out+?, cost=cost+? WHERE id=?",

      159      @staticmethod
      160 +    def _normalise(message: dict, steps: list[dict]) -> dict: 
      161 +        """Make every tool call addressable: a unique id withi
          +n the run and arguments as a JSON string.                     
      162 +        Approvals are keyed by (run, call id), so a reused id 
          +must never inherit an earlier approval."""                    
      163 +        msg = {k: v for k, v in message.items() if v is not No
          +ne}                                                           
      164 +        msg["role"] = "assistant"                             
      165 +        seen = {c["id"] for s in steps if s["kind"] == "llm" f
          +or c in s["data"]["output"].get("tool_calls") or []}          
      166 +        n_llm = sum(1 for s in steps if s["kind"] == "llm") + 
          +1                                                             
      167 +        calls = []                                            
      168 +        for i, c in enumerate(msg.get("tool_calls") or []):   
      169 +            c = {**c, "function": dict(c.get("function") or {}
          +)}                                                            
      170 +            if not c.get("id") or c["id"] in seen:            
      171 +                c["id"] = f"call_s{n_llm}_{i}"                
      172 +            seen.add(c["id"])                                 
      173 +            if not isinstance(c["function"].get("arguments"), 
          +str):                                                         
      174 +                c["function"]["arguments"] = json.dumps(c["fun
          +ction"].get("arguments") or {})                               
      175 +            calls.append(c)                                   
      176 +        if calls:                                             
      177 +            msg["tool_calls"] = calls                         
      178 +        return msg                                            
      179 +                                                              
      180 +    @staticmethod                                             
      181      def _messages(run: dict, steps: list[dict]) -> list[dict]:
      182          msgs = [{"role": "system", "content": SYSTEM}, {"role"
           : "user", "content": run["goal"]}]
      183          return msgs + [s["msg"] for s in steps if s["msg"]]

● The automated security review flagged four issues. All four were real, all are
  fixed, and the suite is now 46 tests, all passing. Nothing is committed yet.

  Issue: Crash recovery with an unreadable audit log
  What could go wrong: After a crash, if the audit log couldn't be read, the
    email or note would be sent again
  Fix: If we can't confirm whether it already happened, don't redo it: record
    an error and tell the agent to escalate
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Issue: Email leak check could be dodged
  What could go wrong: "c-09", fullwidth "Ｃ－０９" or a unicode dash slipped
    past the check for other customers' IDs
  Fix: Normalise case and unicode before matching. Also, the agent can now
    only read the ticket's own customer and payments, so another customer's
    data never enters the conversation at all (this is what defeats the T-103
    attack)
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Issue: Retry-After not capped
  What could go wrong: A huge Retry-After value could stall a run past its
    claim, letting a second worker take it over
  Fix: Capped to 0–30 seconds, and bad values (negative, NaN, text) fall back
    safely
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Issue: Unsafe file paths in export
  What could go wrong: A crafted task ID like ../../x could write files
    outside the export folder
  Fix: Task IDs must look like T-123, and the export refuses any path outside
    its folder

  The read restriction changes agent behaviour. On T-106, the agent can no
  longer look up the colleague's account. That's the right outcome anyway: no
  refund to a different account, escalate to a human.

  Pending decisions for you:
  1. Commit these fixes locally? Message: Harden guards, crash recovery, 
     Retry-After and export paths after security review, under your name only
     and not pushed.
  2. The two emails from the earlier live test still need your approval (the
     commands are in my previous message). If you'd rather not, I'll reset the
     test world later anyway. They're only test runs, not the final graded run.

  After that, I'll build the viewer at localhost:8080.

● Here's where things stand.

  Job 2 (the viewer) is built. Open http://localhost:8080. A live T-101 run is
  paused there waiting for you.

  ┌──────────────┬──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │   Viewer     │                     What you'll see                      │
  │   feature    │                                                          │
  ├──────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Run list     │ Task, status, steps, active time, tokens and cost, start │
  │              │  time                                                    │
  ├──────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │              │ What the model decided and why, every tool call with its │
  │ Run timeline │  arguments and result, per-step latency, and retries and │
  │              │  errors highlighted with each attempt shown              │
  ├──────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Approvals    │ The exact arguments plus the agent's reasoning, a note   │
  │ panel        │ box, and Approve / Reject buttons                        │
  ├──────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Start run    │ Start any task from the dropdown. Replay re-runs a trace │
  │ and Replay   │  with no model and no world, and reports whether it was  │
  │              │ identical                                                │
  ├──────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Without an   │ The page and replay still work, and starting a live run  │
  │ API key      │ is refused with a clear message                          │
  └──────────────┴──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  Every value shown on the page is escaped, because ticket text is written by
  customers and could carry injected HTML.

  Job 3 (human approval) is built into all of this: the pause, approve or reject
  with a note, resume after a restart, and the test that a risky tool can't run
  without approval.

  Other changes since the last push:
  - Security fix: the agent can now only read its own ticket.
  - One-command start: ./run.sh, plus a Dockerfile and docker-compose.yml.
    Docker is untested because it isn't installed here.
  - API tests: the suite is now 50 tests, all passing.

  Please do two things:
  1. Try it: in the viewer, approve the T-101 refund (add a note if you like).
     The run should continue, ask you to approve the email, then finish. This is
     the "approve a risky step" part of your recording.
  2. Commit and push? I'd make two commits under your name only:
     - Scope reads to the run's own ticket (security review)
     - Add viewer: run list, timeline, approvals, start and replay; run.sh and 
       Docker files

  I haven't run ./run.sh end to end yet, because my test server is already using
  port 8080. I'll check it before the final push. Next up: the README (run,
  test and replay instructions, architecture, the honest status section), then
  the final six-task run once the customer email around 14:20 has arrived.

      206 +function toEvents(steps, approvals) {                         
      207 +  const decided = Object.fromEntries(approvals.map(a => [a.cal
          +l_id, a]));                                                   
      208 +  const ev = [];                                              
      209 +  for (const s of steps) {

      211 +    if (s.kind === "llm") {                                   
      212 +      const calls = (d.output.tool_calls || []).map(c => c.fun
          +ction.name);                                                  
      213 +      ev.push({s, lane: "agent", abbr: "AI", status: "ok", tit
          +le: "Agent decided",                                          
      214 +               sub: calls.length ? "→ " + calls.join(", ") : "
          +(answered without a tool)", latency: d.latency_ms});          
      215 +    } else if (s.kind === "tool" && d.name === "finish") {    
      216 +      continue; // shown by the "end" step                    
      217 +    } else if (s.kind === "tool") {                           
      218 +      const tries = (d.attempts || []).filter(a => a.attempt >
          + 0).length;                                                   
      219 +      ev.push({s, lane: RISKY.has(d.name) ? "risk" : ACTS.has(
          +d.name) ? "act" : "read", abbr: ABBR[d.name] || "T",          
      220 +               status: d.status, title: d.name, sub: d.error |
          +| d.reason || "", latency: s.latency_ms, retries: Math.max(0, 
          +tries - 1)});                                                 
      221 +    } else if (s.kind === "approval") {                       
      222 +      const a = decided[d.call_id];                           
      223 +      ev.push({s, lane: "human", abbr: "YOU", status: a ? a.st
          +atus : "pending", title: `Human review: ${d.name}`,           
      224 +               sub: a && a.note ? `“${a.note}”` : (a && a.stat
          +us !== "pending" ? "" : "waiting for a person"), latency: null
          +,                                                             
      225 +               live: !a || a.status === "pending"});          
      226 +    } else if (s.kind === "end") {                            
      227 +      ev.push({s, lane: "end", abbr: "■", status: d.status, ti
          +tle: "Run ended", sub: d.summary, latency: null});            
      228 +    } else if (s.kind === "nudge") {                          
      229 +      ev.push({s, lane: "agent", abbr: "!", status: "blocked",
          + title: "Agent replied without a tool", sub: "reminded to use 
          +tools"});                                                     
      230 +    }                                                         
      231 +  }                                                           
      232 +  return ev;

      308 +function renderChart() {                                      
      309 +  const ev = state.events, LW = 86, LH = 50, X0 = 100, DX = 58
          +;                                                             
      310 +  const w = Math.max(X0 + ev.length * DX + 40, 600), h = LANES
          +.length * LH + 22;                                            
      311 +  const laneY = id => 18 + LANES.findIndex(l => l.id === id) *
          + LH + LH / 2;                                                 
      312 +  const pts = ev.map((e, i) => [X0 + i * DX, laneY(e.lane)]); 
      313 +  let svg = `<svg width="${w}" height="${h}" xmlns="http://www
          +.w3.org/2000/svg">`;                                          
      314 +  LANES.forEach((l, i) => {                                   
      315 +    const y = 18 + i * LH;                                    
      316 +    svg += `<rect x="0" y="${y}" width="${w}" height="${LH}" f
          +ill="${i % 2 ? "#fff" : "transparent"}" opacity=".55"/>`;     
      317 +    svg += `<circle cx="16" cy="${y + LH / 2}" r="4" fill="${l
          +.color}"/><text class="lane-name" x="26" y="${y + LH / 2 + 4}"
          +>${l.name}</text>`;                                           
      318 +  });                                                         
      319 +  svg += `<line x1="${LW}" y1="10" x2="${LW}" y2="${h}" stroke
          +="var(--line)"/>`;

❯ 1. Best libraries for your project                                            
                                                                                
  1. Cytoscape.js — My recommendation                                           
     Best for Flight Recorder                                                   
  - Dynamic nodes and connecting edges.                                         
  - Animate execution from one step to another.                                 
  - Highlight successful, failed and pending steps.                             
  - Show retries and approval checkpoints.                                      
    Documentation                                                               
                                                                                
  2. React Flow                                                                 
  - Beautiful workflow diagrams.                                                
  - Custom nodes for LLM, tools and approvals.                                  
  - Interactive zooming and dragging.                                           
  - Great for a polished dashboard.                                             
    Documentation                                                               
                                                                                
  3. Dash Cytoscape                                                             
  - Python-based interactive graph visualization.                               
  - Less frontend JavaScript required.                                          
  - Integrates with Plotly Dash.                                                
    Documentation                                                               
                                                                                
  4. Plotly                                                                     
  - Token and cost charts.                                                      
  - Execution duration.                                                         
  - Retry counts.                                                               
  - Success and failure statistics.                                             
    Documentation                                                               
    I think something can be used like this for this, because the one that you  
  have created looks a bit ugly. So yeah, you can use one of them, whatever you 
  think is best for it.

● The viewer is rebuilt in your reference's style, with the flight-path chart
  you liked. 52 tests pass. Nothing is committed yet.

  Why you saw those "Failed" runs: all 5 were replays, not real runs.
  - Each Replay click on the old page created a new replay run and jumped to it,
    so clicking Replay again replayed the replay.
  - They failed because the first one was taken in the half-second after your
    Approve, while the original run was still executing it. The replay's
    snapshot didn't yet contain the refund.
  - Your real T-101 run succeeded. The refund was approved and paid once: the
    first attempt got a 504 timeout and the safe retry was answered as
    "replayed". You rejected the email, and the agent then added a note and
    closed the ticket.

  Fixes:
  - Backend: replay now refuses a run that's still executing, and only copies
    human decisions the trace already reflects. Two new tests cover this.
  - Screen: replays no longer clutter the run list (they sit under Replay 
    checks). Verify replay keeps you on the original run, shows a green "✓
    Identical" banner, then plays the run back.

  The new viewer:
  - Layout: the same look as your reference, with three columns (runs, flight
    path, inspector), a two-row header (ticket subject and customer on top,
    stats below) and colour-coded status pills.
  - Flight path: six swimlanes (Agent, Reads, Actions, Risky, Human, Outcome)
    joined by connectors. Errors are marked ✕, policy blocks ⛔, and each retry
    adds a red dot.
  - Playback: ▶ play, step forward and back (← → keys too), a scrubber and
    1×/2×/4× speed. Each step lights up and its details open on the right.
  - Inspector: the "why", the arguments as a clean key–value list, the result,
    and each attempt with a latency bar. Approve and Reject sit at the top when
    a run is waiting.
  - Readable code: no external libraries, so it works offline and is one file to
    read.

  You have T-105 waiting for approval in the viewer now.

  Commit and push? I'd make two commits under your name only. Your ui/ reference
  screenshot stays out of the repo.
  1. Fix replay race and scope reads to the run's own ticket
  2. Add viewer with flight-path playback, approvals and replay checks; run.sh 
     and Docker files

  email around 14:20.

● Agreed: get the brief's requirements working first, UI polish later. Here's
  how I'll handle Ananya's four asks. I'll record all of it in CHANGES.md.

  Ask: 1. Parallel runs, with one company-wide 5/s billing limit
  Decision: Build. A shared limiter for every billing call across all runs,
    set to 4/s for headroom. A 429 pauses all billing callers for its
    Retry-After. Plus a parallel option for runs
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Ask: 2. Kill switch
  Decision: Build. One switch stops every agent at the next safe point, and
    nothing is left half-done. A refund in flight is resolved with its
    idempotency key, so we can report whether it happened. Pending approvals
    are cancelled, and new runs and approvals are refused until the switch is
    released
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Ask: 3. Cost in ₹ and a daily total
  Decision: Build. The exchange rate is configurable
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Ask: 4. Big model for hard tickets, cheap for easy ones
  Decision: Partly. The model becomes selectable per run. No automatic routing
    today: deciding "hard" wrongly would affect correctness, and there's no
    evaluation data yet

  Starting with the limiter and kill switch in the core:

  Read 1 file (ctrl+o to expand)

      58 +def is_billing(tool: dict) -> bool:                            
      59 +    """The manifest marks billing tools in their description ("
         +Billing API: rate limited")."""                                
      60 +    return "billing api" in tool.get("description", "").lower()
      61 +                                                               
      62 +                                                               
      63 +class RateLimiter:                                             
      64 +    """One limit shared by every run in this process: the billi
         +ng API allows 5 calls/s for the WHOLE company.                 
      65 +                                                               
      66 +    Callers reserve evenly spaced slots, so N parallel agents t
         +ogether never exceed the rate. A 429 pushes the next           
      67 +    free slot past its Retry-After for everyone, not just the c
         +aller that hit it.                                             
      68 +    shortcut: in-process only; several worker processes would n
         +eed a shared limiter (e.g. Redis)."""                          
      69 +                                                               
      70 +    def __init__(self, per_second: float, clock: Callable[[], f
         +loat] = time.monotonic,                                        
      71 +                 sleep: Callable[[float], None] = time.sleep): 
      72 +        self.interval, self._clock, self._sleep = 1.0 / per_sec
         +ond, clock, sleep                                              
      73 +        self._next = 0.0                                       
      74 +        self._lock = threading.Lock()                          
      75 +                                                               
      76 +    def acquire(self) -> float:                                
      77 +        with self._lock:                                       
      78 +            now = self._clock()                                
      79 +            slot = max(now, self._next)                        
      80 +            self._next = slot + self.interval                  
      81 +        wait = slot - now                                      
      82 +        if wait > 0:                                           
      83 +            self._sleep(wait)                                  
      84 +        return wait                                            
      85 +                                                               
      86 +    def back_off(self, seconds: float) -> None:                
      87 +        with self._lock:                                       
      88 +            self._next = max(self._next, self._clock() + second
         +s)                                                             
      89 +                                                               
      90 +                                                               
      91  class Gateway:
      59 -    def __init__(self, world: World, manifest: Manifest, sleep:
         - Callable[[float], None] = time.sleep, jitter: bool = True):   
      92 +    def __init__(self, world: World, manifest: Manifest, sleep:
         + Callable[[float], None] = time.sleep, jitter: bool = True,    
      93 +                 limiter: RateLimiter | None = None, stop: Call
         +able[[], bool] = lambda: False):                               
      94          self.world, self.manifest, self._sleep, self._jitter =
          world, manifest, sleep, jitter
      95 +        self.limiter, self.stop = limiter, stop                
      96 
      97      def _wait(self, seconds: float) -> float:
      98          s = seconds + (random.uniform(0, 0.5) if self._jitter e
          lse 0)

        1 +# Flight Recorder — support agents you can trust and explain  
        2 +                                                              
        3 +> **Recording:** _link added after the hard stop_             
        4 +                                                              
        5 +Flight Recorder is a **runtime that runs Northwind's support a
          +gents** and a **viewer that shows exactly what they did       
        6 +and why**. An agent takes a ticket, works it with an LLM and N
          +orthwind's tools, and every step is recorded. Anything that   
        7 +moves money or reaches a customer waits for a person. Any run 
          +can be replayed exactly, with no LLM and no network.          
        8 +                                                              
        9 +The agent loop is written by hand (no agent frameworks). Pytho
          +n 3.12+, FastAPI, SQLite, and a one-file HTML viewer.         
       10 +                                                              
       11 +---                                                           
       12 +                                                              
       13 +## Quick start                                                
       14 +                                                              
       15 +```bash                                                       
       16 +# 1. the Northwind world (separate, as given): http://127.0.0.
          +1:7070                                                        
       17 +python world_server.py                                        
       18 +                                                              
       19 +# 2. runtime + viewer: http://localhost:8080                  
       20 +cp .env.example .env          # add LLM_API_KEY for live runs 
          +(replay works without one)                                    
       21 +./run.sh                      # creates .venv, installs requir
          +ements, starts the viewer                                     
       22 +#   or: docker compose up     (untested here: no Docker on the
          + build machine)                                               
       23 +```                                                           
       24 +                                                              
       25 +Open **http://localhost:8080**, pick a task, and press **Start
          + run**. When the agent wants to refund or email, it           
       26 +pauses and the approval card appears on the right.            
       27 +                                                              
       28 +| Command | What it does |                                    
       29 +|---|---|                                                     
       30 +| `python -m flightrec run T-101 T-102` / `run --all` | Start 
          +runs in parallel. They pause at approvals |                   
       31 +| `python -m flightrec approvals` | List pending approvals (ex
          +act arguments plus the agent's reason) |                      
       32 +| `python -m flightrec approve <run> <call> [note]` / `reject 
          +…` | Decide, then continue the run |                          
       33 +| `python -m flightrec show <run>` | The step-by-step timeline
          + in the terminal |                                            
       34 +| `python -m flightrec replay <run>` | Re-run from the trace w
          +ith no LLM and no world calls, and compare |                  
       35 +| `python -m flightrec stop-all [reason]` / `release` | Kill s
          +witch on and off |                                            
       36 +| `python -m flightrec export runs/final <run>...` | Write the
          + traces and `world_audit.jsonl` |                             
       37 +                                                              
       38 +**Tests:** `pytest`. All 62 run offline in about 10 seconds, w
          +ith no API key and no world server: the world and the         
       39 +model are faked.                                              
       40 +                                                              
       41 +## How it works                                               
       42 +                                                              
       43 +```mermaid                                                    
       44 +flowchart LR                                                  
       45 +    A[Start run<br/>viewer or CLI] --> B[Load tools<br/>GET /t
          +ools]                                                         
       46 +    B --> C{Budget left?<br/>steps · time · tokens}           
       47 +    C -- no --> S1[stopped]                                   
       48 +    C -- yes --> D[LLM decides<br/>step saved]                
       49 +    D --> E{finish called?}                                   
       50 +    E -- yes --> S2[succeeded / failed /<br/>needs_human + sum
          +mary]                                                         
       51 +    E -- no --> F[Validate args<br/>JSON schema + policy guard
          +s]                                                            
       52 +    F -- invalid / blocked --> D                              
       53 +    F --> G{risk: high?}                                      
       54 +    G -- yes --> H[Pause<br/>approval saved]                  
       55 +    H --> I{Human}                                            
       56 +    I -- approve --> J                                        
       57 +    I -- "reject + note" --> D                                
       58 +    G -- no --> J[Call tool<br/>retry policy]                 
       59 +    J --> K[Result saved as a step]                           
       60 +    K --> C                                                   
       61 +```                                                           
       62 +                                                              
       63 +Every step is written to SQLite **before** the next action. Th
          +e loop's state (the conversation, pending calls, guard        
       64 +context) is always rebuilt from those steps, so one code path 
          +covers a fresh run, a restart, an approval that arrives       
       65 +hours later, and a replay.                                    
       66 +                                                              
       67 +```mermaid                                                    
       68 +flowchart TB                                                  
       69 +    subgraph UI["Viewer · localhost:8080 (static/index.html)"]
       70 +        RL[Run list] --- FP[Flight path + playback] --- IN[Ins
          +pector + approvals] --- KS[Kill switch]                       
       71 +    end                                                       
       72 +    CLI[cli.py] --> SVC                                       
       73 +    UI --> API[api.py] --> SVC[service.py<br/>live / replay wi
          +ring, kill switch, export]                                    
       74 +    SVC --> RUN[runner.py<br/>the loop, budgets, approvals, cr
          +ash recovery]                                                 
       75 +    RUN --> LLM[llm.py<br/>OpenAI-compatible client · ReplayLL
          +M]                                                            
       76 +    RUN --> GW[gateway.py<br/>schema validation, retries, idem
          +potency,<br/>shared billing limiter]                          
       77 +    RUN --> POL[policy.py<br/>guards + approval invariant]    
       78 +    RUN --> ST[store.py<br/>SQLite: runs · steps · approvals ·
          + controls]                                                    
       79 +    GW --> W[(Northwind world :7070)]                         
       80 +    LLM --> G[(Gemini 3.5 Flash Lite)]                        
       81 +```                                                           
       82 +                                                              
       83 +| File | Lines | Read it for |                                
       84 +|---|---|---|                                                 
       85 +| `runner.py` | ~380 | The loop: parse → validate → guard → ap
          +proval gate → crash-safe execute |                            
       86 +| `gateway.py` | ~210 | Retry policy by failure type, the shar
          +ed billing rate limiter |                                     
       87 +| `policy.py` | ~95 | Guards that hold even if the model is fo
          +oled, and the approval invariant |                            
       88 +| `store.py` | ~180 | The append-only trace, leases, kill swit
          +ch |                                                          
       89 +| `service.py` · `api.py` · `cli.py` | | Wiring, HTTP API, com
          +mand line |                                                   
       90 +| `static/index.html` | | The viewer (plain JS, no build, ever
          +y value HTML-escaped) |                                       
       91 +                                                              
       92 +## What makes it safe                                         
       93 +                                                              
       94 +| Failure | What happens |                                    
       95 +|---|---|                                                     
       96 +| `429` from billing | Wait for `Retry-After` (capped at 30s).
          + One limiter shared by **all** runs keeps the company under 5 
          +calls/s, and a 429 slows every agent down |                   
       97 +| `5xx`, timeout or broken body on a read | Exponential backof
          +f, at most 6 attempts, each attempt recorded |                
       98 +| `400` / `404` / `422` | Never retried. The error goes back t
          +o the model |                                                 
       99 +| Refund `504` **after** the money moved | Every refund carrie
          +s `idempotency_key = run:payment:amount`, so the retry is answ
          +ered `replayed: true` and nobody is paid twice |              
      100 +| Note or email fails ambiguously | Check `GET /audit` before 
          +retrying. If we can't tell, don't repeat it; escalate |       
      101 +| Crash between "world acted" and "result saved" | An intent s
          +tep is written first. On resume we check the audit instead of 
          +resending |                                                   
      102 +| Model tries a risky tool | It pauses. The executor refuses u
          +nless an **approved record with a matching argument hash** exi
          +sts (tested) |                                                
      103 +| Prompt injection in ticket text (T-103) | Tool output is lab
          +elled untrusted data. Code guards keep reads, refunds and emai
          +ls on the ticket's own customer, and an email can't mention an
          +other customer's IDs |                                        
      104 +| Runaway loop | Step, active-time and token budgets end the r
          +un as `stopped` |                                             
      105 +| Kill switch | Agents stop at the next safe point. Pending ap
          +provals are cancelled, a refund in flight is resolved, and eac
          +h run reports what happened to money |                        
      106 +                                                              
      107 +## My take on Rohan's design                                  
      108 +                                                              
      109 +See [PLAN.md](PLAN.md). In short:                             
      110 +- **Changed:** step limits (we have budgets) and blind 5× imme
          +diate retries (that is the double refund).                    
      111 +- **Half-kept:** JSON traces (they're the export format, but S
          +QLite is the store).                                          
      112 +- **Rejected:** tool output as trusted context (T-103 is the c
          +ounter-example) and the model approving itself                
      113 +  (approval is a human record that code enforces).            
      114 +                                                              
      115 +## Honest status                                              
      116 +                                                              
      117 +- **Works:**                                                  
      118 +  - Six tasks end to end against the world with failures on, r
          +eal model, traces in `runs/final/`.                           
      119 +  - Human approval that survives restarts.                    
      120 +  - Exact replay, the kill switch, and parallel runs under one
          + billing limit.                                               
      121 +  - 62 offline tests. The core safety checks were proven by br
          +eaking them on purpose and watching a test fail.              
      122 +- **Doesn't (yet):**                                          
      123 +  - **No login on the viewer.** It listens on 127.0.0.1 only. 
          +A team needs sign-in and approver identity on every           
      124 +    decision.                                                 
      125 +  - **The limiters are per process.** Several workers would ne
          +ed a shared one, such as Redis.                               
      126 +  - **Docker is untested** (not installed here). `./run.sh` is
          + the tested path.                                             
      127 +- **What the AI got wrong, and how I caught it:**             
      128 +  - It gave every tool a runtime field called `reason`, which 
          +silently deleted the refund's own required `reason`           
      129 +    argument. I caught it reading the manifest, and renamed th
          +e field to `rationale`.                                       
      130 +  - It wrote `\b` inside a non-raw string, so the email-leak r
          +egex had backspace characters and never matched. A test       
      131 +    caught it, and I checked the bytes.                       
      132 +  - Its first replay design copied approvals that the trace di
          +dn't reflect yet. That was a real race, seen in the           
      133 +    viewer; it's fixed and tested.                            
      134 +  - The local server used `localhost`, and every call took 2s 
          +(IPv6 fallback on Windows). Spotted from the latencies        
      135 +    in the first trace.                                       
      136 +- **At 100 concurrent runs:**                                 
      137 +  - The LLM provider's rate limit breaks first: 100 agents sha
          +re one key's requests per minute. The shared LLM              
      138 +    limiter queues them instead of failing, but throughput is 
          +capped by the plan.                                           
      139 +  - Next, SQLite write contention (one writer at a time) and t
          +he in-process thread pool.                                    
      140 +  - The fix: Postgres, a job queue with workers, and Redis-bac
          +ked limiters.                                                 
      141 +- **Next:**                                                   
      142 +  - A playbook of rules reviewed by a person, built from rejec
          +tion notes, with its version recorded per run.                
      143 +  - Automatic "hard vs easy" model routing, once we have outco
          +me data per model.                                            
      144 +  - Authentication and approver identity.                     
      145 +                                                              
      146 +## Configuration                                              
      147 +                                                              
      148 +| Variable | Purpose |                                        
      149 +|---|---|                                                     
      150 +| `LLM_BASE_URL` / `LLM_API_KEY` / `LLM_MODEL` | Any OpenAI-co
          +mpatible endpoint. Without a key, live runs are refused but re
          +play and the viewer work |                                    
      151 +| `LLM_MODELS` | Models an operator may pick per run (comma-se
          +parated; the first is the default) |                          
      152 +| `LLM_PER_MINUTE` | Shared cap on LLM requests across all run
          +s (free tiers limit per key) |                                
      153 +| `WORLD_URL` | The world server (default `http://127.0.0.1:70
          +70`) |                                                        
      154 +| `MAX_STEPS` / `MAX_SECONDS` / `MAX_TOKENS` | Per-run budgets
          + |                                                            
      155 +| `WORKERS` | Runs advanced in parallel |                     
      156 +| `BILLING_PER_SECOND` | Shared billing API rate (default 4, u
          +nder the company limit of 5) |                                
      157 +| `USD_INR` | Rate for showing cost in rupees |               
      158 +| `DB_PATH` | SQLite file (default `data/flightrec.db`) |     
      159 +                                                              
      160 +Changes made during the round are in [CHANGES.md](CHANGES.md).

  Commits: the earlier four commits are pushed under your name only, and the ui/

  Your note on approvals: before, a note on Approve was only stored, and the
  agent never saw it. Approve still runs exactly what you were shown, because an
  approval has to mean that. The card now has three buttons:

  ┌────────────┬────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │   Button   │                        What happens                        │
  ├────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Approve    │ Runs exactly what's shown. Your note is passed to the      │
  │            │ agent too                                                  │
  ├────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Request    │ Nothing runs. Your note ("make it polite") goes to the     │
  │ changes    │ agent, which revises and brings the new version back for   │
  │            │ approval                                                   │
  ├────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Reject     │ Nothing runs. The agent is told not to retry and to find   │
  │            │ another approach                                           │
  └────────────┴────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  "Needs human" now has options. Escalated, failed and stopped runs show a card
  with the agent's reason and the notes it left, plus:
  - Hand back to agent: your guidance starts a follow-up run for the same
    ticket.
  - Mark handled by me: records that you resolved it yourself.

  The brief doesn't spell out what a person should do after an escalation, so
  this fills that gap. I've recorded it in CHANGES.md.

  Dark theme: a ◐ toggle in the top bar, remembered by your browser.

  Security: the viewer now refuses requests addressed to anything other than
  localhost, and refuses POSTs from other websites. This came from the automated
  security review, and it's tested.

  runtime. Adopting it would mean a build step and rewriting a viewer that
  works, with about 80 minutes left. Its agent parts also brush against the
  brief's "no agent frameworks for the loop" rule. It would fit well later as a
  chat where Ananya asks "why did T-106 escalate?" over the traces. I've noted

● The automated security review flagged the new trace-import code, and both
  findings were real. Both are fixed, a test proves it, and 78 tests pass with a
  clean lint.

  - Column names from the file went into SQL. A crafted trace file could inject
    SQL through its keys. Only a fixed list of known columns is used now, and
    unknown keys are ignored.
  - An imported run marked "running" would have been executed for real. The
    janitor would have picked it up and run it against the live world. Only
    finished runs can be imported now.

    skips that file, prints a line about it, and carries on.
